Well, I guess I'm here because... everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure on my house is just, you know, standard paperwork and missed payments. But I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even the people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. 

Speaking of my job, I supervise a retail team, and honestly, the new girl they hired last week? Absolute disaster. Oh, absolutely. It is one hundred percent on purpose. Like I said, they've been planning this and doing things behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to shove these foreclosure papers in my face to make it look legitimate, but I know what I'm seeing. I have an associate's degree in business, okay? I dropped it when I was getting out of my car at the bank last month to try and confront the branch manager. The case I bought for it was supposed to be shatterproof, but that was a total lie. I should probably write a review online about that. 

But yeah, to answer your question, it's definitely a coordinated effort. No amount of fake papers they try to print out and show me is going to change the fact that they are all working together to force me out of my home.

Strangers? Like celebrities or the mayor or something? No, that doesn't make any sense. Why would someone who doesn't even know me care about my house? It's just the people at the bank and my job who are doing this. 

Though speaking of strangers, the customers at the store this week have been completely out of their minds. This one lady came in yesterday demanding a full cash refund for a cardigan she bought three years ago. Three years! She didn't even have the tags or the receipt, and it smelled like mothballs. I had to call mall security because she just planted herself at my register and refused to move. The security guard, Todd, he's actually really nice about that stuff. He brought me a coffee last week from that new cafe down the street, the one with the dark green awning? Their espresso is incredibly bitter, though. I usually prefer making my own at home. I bought this really nice espresso machine on sale last Black Friday, but the milk frother attachment is already acting up. I tried calling their customer service line on my lunch break, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes. Forty-five minutes! Just listening to that awful, static-y elevator music. I don't even know why companies bother having a support line if no one is ever going to pick up the phone. 

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, public figures. But yeah, no, I haven't noticed anyone like that being involved. Just the people I actually deal with.

I need to take them back, but finding the receipt is a whole other project. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no amount of papers they try to show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years now. No, no, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own, and I definitely don't have any special powers. I mean, if I had special powers, I probably would have used them to fix the schedule at work this week.

They don't even stick anymore, the adhesive just turns into this gross, flaky powder. I tried to use one on a papercut I got from opening a shipment of those new denim jackets, and it just fell right off my finger onto the floor. 

So yeah, no mind control or superpowers or whatever. Just me dealing with the same old incompetence from everyone else.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't think things have special secret meanings just for me. A coffee cup is just a coffee cup, you know? Like, I don't look at a billboard or a street lamp and think it's a secret message. 

Although, speaking of signs, the new promotional signage corporate sent us for the autumn clearance sale is completely ridiculous. They printed everything in this blinding neon orange color that clashes horribly with the store's overhead lighting. I told the district manager, Greg, that it makes the whole front section look like a highway construction zone. Greg never listens, though. He was supposed to come in for a walk-through on Tuesday, but he canceled at the last minute because his flight from Chicago got delayed. 

I absolutely hate flying. The last time I was on a plane, the guy next to me kept taking off his shoes, and I swear, his socks smelled exactly like stale corn chips. It was a four-hour flight to Orlando for my cousin's baby shower, and I couldn't even read my book because I was so nauseous. I ended up buying her this really expensive baby monitor off her registry, one of those ones with the HD video and room temperature sensors. It cost me like two hundred dollars, and then she didn't even send a thank-you note. Just a mass text to everyone with a picture of the baby's foot. I mean, who does that? It takes two seconds to write a card. I buy these beautiful embossed cards from this little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that makes those incredible almond croissants... 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, special meanings. No, everything is just what it is. Nothing weird like that. Except the foreclosure papers, obviously, but that's just them being corrupt, not some magic symbol.

I ended up buying her this really expensive baby monitor off her registry, one of those ones with the HD video and room temperature sensors. Control people? No, definitely not. God, if I could control people, my life would be so much easier. I would definitely make my cashier, Jason, actually show up for his shifts on time. 

He was twenty minutes late again this morning because he claimed he had to get gas. I mean, who waits until right before they have to be at work to get gas? You do it the night before. I always go to that Shell station on 4th Street because they usually have the cheapest unleaded, though their squeegees are always completely ruined. The sponge part is always ripped off, so it just scrapes the glass with the hard plastic. Screech, screech. It drives me crazy. I absolutely hate a dirty windshield, especially with the glare from the sun this time of year. The sun sets so early now, it's pitch black by the time I drive home. 

I actually had to change the headlight bulb on my Honda last weekend and it was a total nightmare. You practically have to take the whole front bumper off just to reach the housing unit. My hands were covered in grease for two days, no matter how much Dawn dish soap I used. Dawn is usually so good, too. I use it on everything, even laundry stains. If you mix it with a little baking soda, it takes tomato sauce right out of cotton. I spilled spaghetti on my favorite white blouse last month at that Italian place downtown—the one with the red checkered tablecloths? Their garlic bread is incredibly dry, honestly. It's basically toast. 

Wait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, making people do things without them knowing. No, I can't do that. I can't even get Jason to wear his nametag, let alone control his mind.

Fear or doom? No, not really. I mean, I get stressed, obviously, but not like a sudden panic out of nowhere for no reason. 

Although, speaking of bad things happening, the roof at the store started leaking again yesterday. Right over the clearance shoe rack. It's ridiculous. I've put in three maintenance requests this month alone, and corporate just keeps sending this guy, Gary, who basically slaps some duct tape on a pipe and calls it a day. Gary always smells like stale cigarettes and peppermint breath mints, it's the weirdest combination. He drives this beat-up old Ford truck that takes up two parking spaces out back. 

I actually got a huge dent in my car door last year from someone parking too close to me at the grocery store. I came out with my bags—I had just bought all the ingredients to make a lasagna, the really good kind with the ricotta cheese and the spicy Italian sausage. You have to brown the sausage first, obviously, but my sister always skips that step and just throws it in raw, and it makes the whole thing so greasy. She invited me over for dinner last Thanksgiving and the turkey was so dry it was like chewing on sawdust. She didn't even make her own cranberry sauce, just bought the canned stuff that keeps the shape of the tin when you slide it out. I hate that. 

Anyway, I came out of the grocery store and there was this massive dent right on the driver's side. Cost me four hundred dollars to get it popped out at the body shop on Elm Street. The guy there, Tony, he's actually really nice. He gave me a discount because I paid in cash. I always try to keep a little cash on me, just in case. You never know when the card readers are going to go down. Ours at the store went down for two hours last Black Friday, and people were absolutely losing their minds. 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, a sense of doom. No, no random doom. Just the normal stress of keeping the store from falling apart.

Absolute disaster. She can't even fold the sweaters right on the display tables, and I had to stay an hour late on Tuesday just re-folding everything. My feet were absolutely killing me. I bought these new shoes that were supposed to have memory foam, but I swear they're defective or something. I need to take them back, but finding the receipt is a whole other project. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no amount of papers they try to show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years now. They think I don't notice what they're doing, but I do. Just like I notice when the delivery guys leave the boxes in the wrong loading zone at the store. You really can't trust anyone to just do their job properly anymore, can you?\n\nDoctor: When you say the bank, the loan servicer, and people at your job are working together to push you out of your house, do you believe this is happening on purpose, like a planned effort against you?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It is one hundred percent on purpose. Like I said, they've been planning this and doing things behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to shove these foreclosure papers in my face to make it look legitimate, but I know what I'm seeing. I have an associate's degree in business, okay? I know how paperwork can be manipulated. \n\nWhich reminds me, I had to review the vendor invoices at the store yesterday because my manager—who is definitely in on the house thing, by the way—tried to say we were over budget on the seasonal displays. We ordered the spiced pumpkin candles, but the warehouse sent vanilla bean. Who even buys vanilla bean in October? It completely throws off the autumn aesthetic I had planned for the front window. I spent three hours sketching out that display on my iPad. My iPad screen actually has a crack in it right down the middle now... I dropped it when I was getting out of my car at the bank last month to try and confront the branch manager. The case I bought for it was supposed to be shatterproof, but that was a total lie. I should probably write a review online about that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, it's definitely a coordinated effort. No amount of fake papers they try to print out and show me is going to change the fact that they are all working together to force me out of my home.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people who are not directly involved in your job or the foreclosure, like strangers or public figures, are also part of this effort against you?\n\nPatient: Strangers? Like celebrities or the mayor or something? No, that doesn't make any sense. Why would someone who doesn't even know me care about my house? It's just the people at the bank and my job who are doing this. \n\nThough speaking of strangers, the customers at the store this week have been completely out of their minds. This one lady came in yesterday demanding a full cash refund for a cardigan she bought three years ago. Three years! She didn't even have the tags or the receipt, and it smelled like mothballs. I had to call mall security because she just planted herself at my register and refused to move. The security guard, Todd, he's actually really nice about that stuff. He brought me a coffee last week from that new cafe down the street, the one with the dark green awning? Their espresso is incredibly bitter, though. I usually prefer making my own at home. I bought this really nice espresso machine on sale last Black Friday, but the milk frother attachment is already acting up. I tried calling their customer service line on my lunch break, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes. Forty-five minutes! Just listening to that awful, static-y elevator music. I don't even know why companies bother having a support line if no one is ever going to pick up the phone. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, public figures. But yeah, no, I haven't noticed anyone like that being involved. Just the people I actually deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something unusual was happening with your thoughts, like someone putting thoughts in or out of your mind, or that you had special powers or abilities that others don’t know about?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own, and I definitely don't have any special powers. I mean, if I had special powers, I probably would have used them to fix the schedule at work this week. \n\nMy assistant manager, Sarah, she completely messed up the shift rotations. She put three cashiers on during the slow morning shift and only one during the afternoon rush. It was a nightmare. I had to jump on a register myself for three hours straight, and the barcode scanner at register two is so finicky. You have to hold the tag at this exact, weird angle or it just beeps at you. Beep, beep, beep. It gave me such a headache. I ended up taking some ibuprofen from the first aid kit in the breakroom, but I think it was expired because it didn't help at all. I really need to remember to check the expiration dates on that stuff. Last time I looked, we still had a box of band-aids from like, 2018. They don't even stick anymore, the adhesive just turns into this gross, flaky powder. I tried to use one on a papercut I got from opening a shipment of those new denim jackets, and it just fell right off my finger onto the floor. \n\nSo yeah, no mind control or superpowers or whatever. Just me dealing with the same old incompetence from everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, objects, or actions in your environment had a special meaning specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think things have special secret meanings just for me. A coffee cup is just a coffee cup, you know? Like, I don't look at a billboard or a street lamp and think it's a secret message. \n\nAlthough, speaking of signs, the new promotional signage corporate sent us for the autumn clearance sale is completely ridiculous. They printed everything in this blinding neon orange color that clashes horribly with the store's overhead lighting. I told the district manager, Greg, that it makes the whole front section look like a highway construction zone. Greg never listens, though. He was supposed to come in for a walk-through on Tuesday, but he canceled at the last minute because his flight from Chicago got delayed. \n\nI absolutely hate flying. The last time I was on a plane, the guy next to me kept taking off his shoes, and I swear, his socks smelled exactly like stale corn chips. It was a four-hour flight to Orlando for my cousin's baby shower, and I couldn't even read my book because I was so nauseous. I ended up buying her this really expensive baby monitor off her registry, one of those ones with the HD video and room temperature sensors. It cost me like two hundred dollars, and then she didn't even send a thank-you note. Just a mass text to everyone with a picture of the baby's foot. I mean, who does that? It takes two seconds to write a card. I buy these beautiful embossed cards from this little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that makes those incredible almond croissants... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, special meanings. No, everything is just what it is. Nothing weird like that. Except the foreclosure papers, obviously, but that's just them being corrupt, not some magic symbol.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you had control or influence over other people’s actions or decisions, like you could affect how they behaved without them realizing it?\n\nPatient: Control people? No, definitely not. God, if I could control people, my life would be so much easier. I would definitely make my cashier, Jason, actually show up for his shifts on time. \n\nHe was twenty minutes late again this morning because he claimed he had to get gas. I mean, who waits until right before they have to be at work to get gas? You do it the night before. I always go to that Shell station on 4th Street because they usually have the cheapest unleaded, though their squeegees are always completely ruined. The sponge part is always ripped off, so it just scrapes the glass with the hard plastic. Screech, screech. It drives me crazy. I absolutely hate a dirty windshield, especially with the glare from the sun this time of year. The sun sets so early now, it's pitch black by the time I drive home. \n\nI actually had to change the headlight bulb on my Honda last weekend and it was a total nightmare. You practically have to take the whole front bumper off just to reach the housing unit. My hands were covered in grease for two days, no matter how much Dawn dish soap I used. Dawn is usually so good, too. I use it on everything, even laundry stains. If you mix it with a little baking soda, it takes tomato sauce right out of cotton. I spilled spaghetti on my favorite white blouse last month at that Italian place downtown—the one with the red checkered tablecloths? Their garlic bread is incredibly dry, honestly. It's basically toast. \n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, making people do things without them knowing. No, I can't do that. I can't even get Jason to wear his nametag, let alone control his mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a sudden sense of fear or doom without knowing exactly why, like something bad was going to happen but there was no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Fear or doom? No, not really. I mean, I get stressed, obviously, but not like a sudden panic out of nowhere for no reason. \n\nAlthough, speaking of bad things happening, the roof at the store started leaking again yesterday. Right over the clearance shoe rack. It's ridiculous. I've put in three maintenance requests this month alone, and corporate just keeps sending this guy, Gary, who basically slaps some duct tape on a pipe and calls it a day. Gary always smells like stale cigarettes and peppermint breath mints, it's the weirdest combination. He drives this beat-up old Ford truck that takes up two parking spaces out back. \n\nI actually got a huge dent in my car door last year from someone parking too close to me at the grocery store. I came out with my bags—I had just bought all the ingredients to make a lasagna, the really good kind with the ricotta cheese and the spicy Italian sausage. You have to brown the sausage first, obviously, but my sister always skips that step and just throws it in raw, and it makes the whole thing so greasy. She invited me over for dinner last Thanksgiving and the turkey was so dry it was like chewing on sawdust. She didn't even make her own cranberry sauce, just bought the canned stuff that keeps the shape of the tin when you slide it out. I hate that. \n\nAnyway, I came out of the grocery store and there was this massive dent right on the driver's side. Cost me four hundred dollars to get it popped out at the body shop on Elm Street. The guy there, Tony, he's actually really nice. He gave me a discount because I paid in cash. I always try to keep a little cash on me, just in case. You never know when the card readers are going to go down. Ours at the store went down for two hours last Black Friday, and people were absolutely losing their minds. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, a sense of doom. No, no random doom. Just the normal stress of keeping the store from falling apart.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

```json
{"candidates": ["F22", "F20", "F24"], "note": "No significant new information to rule out or add any candidates this turn.", "is_final": false}
```

Well, yes, actually. That's exactly why I'm here. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just standard paperwork and missed payments. 

Speaking of paperwork, the new inventory logs corporate wants us to fill out are a complete waste of time. We have to count every single item in the overstock bins manually now instead of just using the scanner. I was back there for three hours on Wednesday, and it is absolutely freezing in the stockroom because the weather stripping on the receiving door is completely rotted out. I told Greg we need a new rubber seal, but he just ignores me. He’s probably too busy eating those awful powdered donuts he always brings in. The white powder gets everywhere—all over his keyboard, all over the desk in the back office. It’s disgusting. I have to wipe the whole area down with Clorox wipes before I can even sit down to do the weekly schedule. I only buy the lemon-scented wipes, though, because the "fresh breeze" scent gives me a terrible headache. I get these awful tension headaches right behind my eyes, especially when the pollen count is high. 

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, groups targeting me. Yes. Like I said, it's not just missed payments like everyone claims. I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. They've been coordinating this whole thing behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to hand me all these official-looking documents to prove I'm behind, but no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. I know what they're doing.

He brought me a coffee last week from that new cafe down the street, the one with the dark green awning? Their espresso is incredibly bitter, though. I usually prefer making my own at home. I bought this really nice espresso machine on sale last Black Friday, but the milk frother attachment is already acting up. I tried calling their customer service line on my lunch break, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes. Forty-five minutes! Just listening to that awful, static-y elevator music. I don't even know why companies bother having a support line if no one is ever going to pick up the phone. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, public figures. But yeah, no, I haven't noticed anyone like that being involved. Just the people I actually deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something unusual was happening with your thoughts, like someone putting thoughts in or out of your mind, or that you had special powers or abilities that others don’t know about?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own, and I definitely don't have any special powers. I mean, if I had special powers, I probably would have used them to fix the schedule at work this week. \n\nMy assistant manager, Sarah, she completely messed up the shift rotations. She put three cashiers on during the slow morning shift and only one during the afternoon rush. It was a nightmare. I had to jump on a register myself for three hours straight, and the barcode scanner at register two is so finicky. You have to hold the tag at this exact, weird angle or it just beeps at you. Beep, beep, beep. It gave me such a headache. I ended up taking some ibuprofen from the first aid kit in the breakroom, but I think it was expired because it didn't help at all. I really need to remember to check the expiration dates on that stuff. Last time I looked, we still had a box of band-aids from like, 2018. They don't even stick anymore, the adhesive just turns into this gross, flaky powder. I tried to use one on a papercut I got from opening a shipment of those new denim jackets, and it just fell right off my finger onto the floor. \n\nSo yeah, no mind control or superpowers or whatever. Just me dealing with the same old incompetence from everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, objects, or actions in your environment had a special meaning specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think things have special secret meanings just for me. A coffee cup is just a coffee cup, you know? Like, I don't look at a billboard or a street lamp and think it's a secret message. \n\nAlthough, speaking of signs, the new promotional signage corporate sent us for the autumn clearance sale is completely ridiculous. They printed everything in this blinding neon orange color that clashes horribly with the store's overhead lighting. I told the district manager, Greg, that it makes the whole front section look like a highway construction zone. Greg never listens, though. He was supposed to come in for a walk-through on Tuesday, but he canceled at the last minute because his flight from Chicago got delayed. \n\nI absolutely hate flying. The last time I was on a plane, the guy next to me kept taking off his shoes, and I swear, his socks smelled exactly like stale corn chips. It was a four-hour flight to Orlando for my cousin's baby shower, and I couldn't even read my book because I was so nauseous. I ended up buying her this really expensive baby monitor off her registry, one of those ones with the HD video and room temperature sensors. It cost me like two hundred dollars, and then she didn't even send a thank-you note. Just a mass text to everyone with a picture of the baby's foot. I mean, who does that? It takes two seconds to write a card. I buy these beautiful embossed cards from this little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that makes those incredible almond croissants... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, special meanings. No, everything is just what it is. Nothing weird like that. Except the foreclosure papers, obviously, but that's just them being corrupt, not some magic symbol.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you had control or influence over other people’s actions or decisions, like you could affect how they behaved without them realizing it?\n\nPatient: Control people? No, definitely not. God, if I could control people, my life would be so much easier. I would definitely make my cashier, Jason, actually show up for his shifts on time. \n\nHe was twenty minutes late again this morning because he claimed he had to get gas. I mean, who waits until right before they have to be at work to get gas? You do it the night before. I always go to that Shell station on 4th Street because they usually have the cheapest unleaded, though their squeegees are always completely ruined. The sponge part is always ripped off, so it just scrapes the glass with the hard plastic. Screech, screech. It drives me crazy. I absolutely hate a dirty windshield, especially with the glare from the sun this time of year. The sun sets so early now, it's pitch black by the time I drive home. \n\nI actually had to change the headlight bulb on my Honda last weekend and it was a total nightmare. You practically have to take the whole front bumper off just to reach the housing unit. My hands were covered in grease for two days, no matter how much Dawn dish soap I used. Dawn is usually so good, too. I use it on everything, even laundry stains. If you mix it with a little baking soda, it takes tomato sauce right out of cotton. I spilled spaghetti on my favorite white blouse last month at that Italian place downtown—the one with the red checkered tablecloths? Their garlic bread is incredibly dry, honestly. It's basically toast. \n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, making people do things without them knowing. No, I can't do that. I can't even get Jason to wear his nametag, let alone control his mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a sudden sense of fear or doom without knowing exactly why, like something bad was going to happen but there was no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Fear or doom? No, not really. I mean, I get stressed, obviously, but not like a sudden panic out of nowhere for no reason. \n\nAlthough, speaking of bad things happening, the roof at the store started leaking again yesterday. Right over the clearance shoe rack. It's ridiculous. I've put in three maintenance requests this month alone, and corporate just keeps sending this guy, Gary, who basically slaps some duct tape on a pipe and calls it a day. Gary always smells like stale cigarettes and peppermint breath mints, it's the weirdest combination. He drives this beat-up old Ford truck that takes up two parking spaces out back. \n\nI actually got a huge dent in my car door last year from someone parking too close to me at the grocery store. I came out with my bags—I had just bought all the ingredients to make a lasagna, the really good kind with the ricotta cheese and the spicy Italian sausage. You have to brown the sausage first, obviously, but my sister always skips that step and just throws it in raw, and it makes the whole thing so greasy. She invited me over for dinner last Thanksgiving and the turkey was so dry it was like chewing on sawdust. She didn't even make her own cranberry sauce, just bought the canned stuff that keeps the shape of the tin when you slide it out. I hate that. \n\nAnyway, I came out of the grocery store and there was this massive dent right on the driver's side. Cost me four hundred dollars to get it popped out at the body shop on Elm Street. The guy there, Tony, he's actually really nice. He gave me a discount because I paid in cash. I always try to keep a little cash on me, just in case. You never know when the card readers are going to go down. Ours at the store went down for two hours last Black Friday, and people were absolutely losing their minds. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, a sense of doom. No, no random doom. Just the normal stress of keeping the store from falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that larger, organized groups, like government agencies or corporations, were specifically targeting you or involved in efforts against you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. That's exactly why I'm here. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just standard paperwork and missed payments. \n\nSpeaking of paperwork, the new inventory logs corporate wants us to fill out are a complete waste of time. We have to count every single item in the overstock bins manually now instead of just using the scanner. I was back there for three hours on Wednesday, and it is absolutely freezing in the stockroom because the weather stripping on the receiving door is completely rotted out. I told Greg we need a new rubber seal, but he just ignores me. He’s probably too busy eating those awful powdered donuts he always brings in. The white powder gets everywhere—all over his keyboard, all over the desk in the back office. It’s disgusting. I have to wipe the whole area down with Clorox wipes before I can even sit down to do the weekly schedule. I only buy the lemon-scented wipes, though, because the \"fresh breeze\" scent gives me a terrible headache. I get these awful tension headaches right behind my eyes, especially when the pollen count is high. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, groups targeting me. Yes. Like I said, it's not just missed payments like everyone claims. I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. They've been coordinating this whole thing behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to hand me all these official-looking documents to prove I'm behind, but no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. I know what they're doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

No, why would I? I told you, I know exactly what I've been seeing for the last two years. The bank, the loan servicer, my coworkers—they are all in on it. They're working together to push me out of my house, and I don't care how many fake documents they try to hand me about missed payments.

I just got my nails done on Tuesday, this really pretty pale pink color. The girl who does them, Sarah, she’s amazing, but she talks so much. She told me her entire life story while she was doing my cuticles. Apparently, her boyfriend just bought a motorcycle, one of those loud Harley Davidsons, and she hates it because it messes up her hair when she rides on the back. I don't blame her. I rode on a motorcycle once in high school and it was terrifying. You feel completely exposed, like one wrong move and you're roadkill. Plus, the bugs. You wouldn't believe how many bugs hit you in the face if you don't wear a full helmet. 

Wait, what were you asking? Oh, if I think I'm wrong about the bank and everything. No, absolutely not. Like I said, no amount of paperwork is going to convince me this is just a normal foreclosure. I know what's really going on.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because... everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure on my house is just, you know, standard paperwork and missed payments. But I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even the people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. \n\nSpeaking of my job, I supervise a retail team, and honestly, the new girl they hired last week? Absolute disaster. She can't even fold the sweaters right on the display tables, and I had to stay an hour late on Tuesday just re-folding everything. My feet were absolutely killing me. I bought these new shoes that were supposed to have memory foam, but I swear they're defective or something. I need to take them back, but finding the receipt is a whole other project. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no amount of papers they try to show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years now. They think I don't notice what they're doing, but I do. Just like I notice when the delivery guys leave the boxes in the wrong loading zone at the store. You really can't trust anyone to just do their job properly anymore, can you?\n\nDoctor: When you say the bank, the loan servicer, and people at your job are working together to push you out of your house, do you believe this is happening on purpose, like a planned effort against you?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It is one hundred percent on purpose. Like I said, they've been planning this and doing things behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to shove these foreclosure papers in my face to make it look legitimate, but I know what I'm seeing. I have an associate's degree in business, okay? I know how paperwork can be manipulated. \n\nWhich reminds me, I had to review the vendor invoices at the store yesterday because my manager—who is definitely in on the house thing, by the way—tried to say we were over budget on the seasonal displays. We ordered the spiced pumpkin candles, but the warehouse sent vanilla bean. Who even buys vanilla bean in October? It completely throws off the autumn aesthetic I had planned for the front window. I spent three hours sketching out that display on my iPad. My iPad screen actually has a crack in it right down the middle now... I dropped it when I was getting out of my car at the bank last month to try and confront the branch manager. The case I bought for it was supposed to be shatterproof, but that was a total lie. I should probably write a review online about that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, it's definitely a coordinated effort. No amount of fake papers they try to print out and show me is going to change the fact that they are all working together to force me out of my home.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people who are not directly involved in your job or the foreclosure, like strangers or public figures, are also part of this effort against you?\n\nPatient: Strangers? Like celebrities or the mayor or something? No, that doesn't make any sense. Why would someone who doesn't even know me care about my house? It's just the people at the bank and my job who are doing this. \n\nThough speaking of strangers, the customers at the store this week have been completely out of their minds. This one lady came in yesterday demanding a full cash refund for a cardigan she bought three years ago. Three years! She didn't even have the tags or the receipt, and it smelled like mothballs. I had to call mall security because she just planted herself at my register and refused to move. The security guard, Todd, he's actually really nice about that stuff. He brought me a coffee last week from that new cafe down the street, the one with the dark green awning? Their espresso is incredibly bitter, though. I usually prefer making my own at home. I bought this really nice espresso machine on sale last Black Friday, but the milk frother attachment is already acting up. I tried calling their customer service line on my lunch break, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes. Forty-five minutes! Just listening to that awful, static-y elevator music. I don't even know why companies bother having a support line if no one is ever going to pick up the phone. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, public figures. But yeah, no, I haven't noticed anyone like that being involved. Just the people I actually deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something unusual was happening with your thoughts, like someone putting thoughts in or out of your mind, or that you had special powers or abilities that others don’t know about?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own, and I definitely don't have any special powers. I mean, if I had special powers, I probably would have used them to fix the schedule at work this week. \n\nMy assistant manager, Sarah, she completely messed up the shift rotations. She put three cashiers on during the slow morning shift and only one during the afternoon rush. It was a nightmare. I had to jump on a register myself for three hours straight, and the barcode scanner at register two is so finicky. You have to hold the tag at this exact, weird angle or it just beeps at you. Beep, beep, beep. It gave me such a headache. I ended up taking some ibuprofen from the first aid kit in the breakroom, but I think it was expired because it didn't help at all. I really need to remember to check the expiration dates on that stuff. Last time I looked, we still had a box of band-aids from like, 2018. They don't even stick anymore, the adhesive just turns into this gross, flaky powder. I tried to use one on a papercut I got from opening a shipment of those new denim jackets, and it just fell right off my finger onto the floor. \n\nSo yeah, no mind control or superpowers or whatever. Just me dealing with the same old incompetence from everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, objects, or actions in your environment had a special meaning specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think things have special secret meanings just for me. A coffee cup is just a coffee cup, you know? Like, I don't look at a billboard or a street lamp and think it's a secret message. \n\nAlthough, speaking of signs, the new promotional signage corporate sent us for the autumn clearance sale is completely ridiculous. They printed everything in this blinding neon orange color that clashes horribly with the store's overhead lighting. I told the district manager, Greg, that it makes the whole front section look like a highway construction zone. Greg never listens, though. He was supposed to come in for a walk-through on Tuesday, but he canceled at the last minute because his flight from Chicago got delayed. \n\nI absolutely hate flying. The last time I was on a plane, the guy next to me kept taking off his shoes, and I swear, his socks smelled exactly like stale corn chips. It was a four-hour flight to Orlando for my cousin's baby shower, and I couldn't even read my book because I was so nauseous. I ended up buying her this really expensive baby monitor off her registry, one of those ones with the HD video and room temperature sensors. It cost me like two hundred dollars, and then she didn't even send a thank-you note. Just a mass text to everyone with a picture of the baby's foot. I mean, who does that? It takes two seconds to write a card. I buy these beautiful embossed cards from this little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that makes those incredible almond croissants... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, special meanings. No, everything is just what it is. Nothing weird like that. Except the foreclosure papers, obviously, but that's just them being corrupt, not some magic symbol.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you had control or influence over other people’s actions or decisions, like you could affect how they behaved without them realizing it?\n\nPatient: Control people? No, definitely not. God, if I could control people, my life would be so much easier. I would definitely make my cashier, Jason, actually show up for his shifts on time. \n\nHe was twenty minutes late again this morning because he claimed he had to get gas. I mean, who waits until right before they have to be at work to get gas? You do it the night before. I always go to that Shell station on 4th Street because they usually have the cheapest unleaded, though their squeegees are always completely ruined. The sponge part is always ripped off, so it just scrapes the glass with the hard plastic. Screech, screech. It drives me crazy. I absolutely hate a dirty windshield, especially with the glare from the sun this time of year. The sun sets so early now, it's pitch black by the time I drive home. \n\nI actually had to change the headlight bulb on my Honda last weekend and it was a total nightmare. You practically have to take the whole front bumper off just to reach the housing unit. My hands were covered in grease for two days, no matter how much Dawn dish soap I used. Dawn is usually so good, too. I use it on everything, even laundry stains. If you mix it with a little baking soda, it takes tomato sauce right out of cotton. I spilled spaghetti on my favorite white blouse last month at that Italian place downtown—the one with the red checkered tablecloths? Their garlic bread is incredibly dry, honestly. It's basically toast. \n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, making people do things without them knowing. No, I can't do that. I can't even get Jason to wear his nametag, let alone control his mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a sudden sense of fear or doom without knowing exactly why, like something bad was going to happen but there was no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Fear or doom? No, not really. I mean, I get stressed, obviously, but not like a sudden panic out of nowhere for no reason. \n\nAlthough, speaking of bad things happening, the roof at the store started leaking again yesterday. Right over the clearance shoe rack. It's ridiculous. I've put in three maintenance requests this month alone, and corporate just keeps sending this guy, Gary, who basically slaps some duct tape on a pipe and calls it a day. Gary always smells like stale cigarettes and peppermint breath mints, it's the weirdest combination. He drives this beat-up old Ford truck that takes up two parking spaces out back. \n\nI actually got a huge dent in my car door last year from someone parking too close to me at the grocery store. I came out with my bags—I had just bought all the ingredients to make a lasagna, the really good kind with the ricotta cheese and the spicy Italian sausage. You have to brown the sausage first, obviously, but my sister always skips that step and just throws it in raw, and it makes the whole thing so greasy. She invited me over for dinner last Thanksgiving and the turkey was so dry it was like chewing on sawdust. She didn't even make her own cranberry sauce, just bought the canned stuff that keeps the shape of the tin when you slide it out. I hate that. \n\nAnyway, I came out of the grocery store and there was this massive dent right on the driver's side. Cost me four hundred dollars to get it popped out at the body shop on Elm Street. The guy there, Tony, he's actually really nice. He gave me a discount because I paid in cash. I always try to keep a little cash on me, just in case. You never know when the card readers are going to go down. Ours at the store went down for two hours last Black Friday, and people were absolutely losing their minds. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, a sense of doom. No, no random doom. Just the normal stress of keeping the store from falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that larger, organized groups, like government agencies or corporations, were specifically targeting you or involved in efforts against you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. That's exactly why I'm here. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just standard paperwork and missed payments. \n\nSpeaking of paperwork, the new inventory logs corporate wants us to fill out are a complete waste of time. We have to count every single item in the overstock bins manually now instead of just using the scanner. I was back there for three hours on Wednesday, and it is absolutely freezing in the stockroom because the weather stripping on the receiving door is completely rotted out. I told Greg we need a new rubber seal, but he just ignores me. He’s probably too busy eating those awful powdered donuts he always brings in. The white powder gets everywhere—all over his keyboard, all over the desk in the back office. It’s disgusting. I have to wipe the whole area down with Clorox wipes before I can even sit down to do the weekly schedule. I only buy the lemon-scented wipes, though, because the \"fresh breeze\" scent gives me a terrible headache. I get these awful tension headaches right behind my eyes, especially when the pollen count is high. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, groups targeting me. Yes. Like I said, it's not just missed payments like everyone claims. I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. They've been coordinating this whole thing behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to hand me all these official-looking documents to prove I'm behind, but no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. I know what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the evidence you're seeing might not actually support the idea that people are working against you, even though it feels that way to you?\n\nPatient: No, why would I? I told you, I know exactly what I've been seeing for the last two years. The bank, the loan servicer, my coworkers—they are all in on it. They're working together to push me out of my house, and I don't care how many fake documents they try to hand me about missed payments. \n\nSpeaking of missing things, Jason actually lost the keys to the display cases yesterday. Just completely misplaced them. We spent forty-five minutes looking for them, and they were sitting right on top of the microwave in the breakroom the whole time. Who puts keys on a microwave? It’s completely unsanitary. The inside of that microwave is a biohazard anyway because nobody ever covers their food. Someone exploded a Hot Pocket in there last week and just left it. The cheese was baked onto the glass plate like cement. I had to scrub it with one of those green Scotch-Brite pads, which I hate using because they ruin my manicures. \n\nI just got my nails done on Tuesday, this really pretty pale pink color. The girl who does them, Sarah, she’s amazing, but she talks so much. She told me her entire life story while she was doing my cuticles. Apparently, her boyfriend just bought a motorcycle, one of those loud Harley Davidsons, and she hates it because it messes up her hair when she rides on the back. I don't blame her. I rode on a motorcycle once in high school and it was terrifying. You feel completely exposed, like one wrong move and you're roadkill. Plus, the bugs. You wouldn't believe how many bugs hit you in the face if you don't wear a full helmet. \n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, if I think I'm wrong about the bank and everything. No, absolutely not. Like I said, no amount of paperwork is going to convince me this is just a normal foreclosure. I know what's really going on.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

  }

A reason? Of course they have a reason. You don't spend almost two years quietly working together behind someone's back to push them out of their house just for fun. They want the property. 

It’s a really beautiful lot, actually. I have these massive hydrangea bushes all along the front porch. You have to mix coffee grounds into the soil if you want the blooms to stay that really deep, vibrant blue. If the soil gets too alkaline, they turn pink, and I absolutely despise pink flowers in the front yard. It completely clashes with the brick on the house. I usually get my used coffee grounds from the Starbucks down the street—they give away those silver bags of espresso grounds for free if you ask. But the new manager there, this kid named Tyler, he started telling me I could only take one bag at a time. One bag! Do you know how big my flower beds are? 

Tyler has this awful little mustache that looks like he drew it on with a Sharpie, and he always wears his green apron tied way too tight. It makes him look like a stuffed sausage. I complained to the district manager about him, but you know how food service is. They never fire anyone unless they actually steal from the register. My cousin worked at a diner in Chicago a few years ago and told me the cooks used to drop food on the floor and just put it right back on the plate. That's exactly why I never eat at diners anymore. The floors are always so sticky, too. Like, just mop your floors, it's really not that hard. We mop the breakroom at my store every single night. Well, we do when Jason actually remembers to do his closing duties instead of playing on his phone in the bathroom for twenty minutes. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, why they're doing it. Like I’ve been saying, everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just standard paperwork and missed payments. But I know what I've been seeing for almost two years now. The bank, the loan servicer, the people at my job—they are all quietly working together to get me out of my house so they can take it. And no amount of papers they try to show me is going to change that. I know exactly what they're up to.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20, F24\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because... everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure on my house is just, you know, standard paperwork and missed payments. But I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even the people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. \n\nSpeaking of my job, I supervise a retail team, and honestly, the new girl they hired last week? Absolute disaster. She can't even fold the sweaters right on the display tables, and I had to stay an hour late on Tuesday just re-folding everything. My feet were absolutely killing me. I bought these new shoes that were supposed to have memory foam, but I swear they're defective or something. I need to take them back, but finding the receipt is a whole other project. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, no amount of papers they try to show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years now. They think I don't notice what they're doing, but I do. Just like I notice when the delivery guys leave the boxes in the wrong loading zone at the store. You really can't trust anyone to just do their job properly anymore, can you?\n\nDoctor: When you say the bank, the loan servicer, and people at your job are working together to push you out of your house, do you believe this is happening on purpose, like a planned effort against you?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. It is one hundred percent on purpose. Like I said, they've been planning this and doing things behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to shove these foreclosure papers in my face to make it look legitimate, but I know what I'm seeing. I have an associate's degree in business, okay? I know how paperwork can be manipulated. \n\nWhich reminds me, I had to review the vendor invoices at the store yesterday because my manager—who is definitely in on the house thing, by the way—tried to say we were over budget on the seasonal displays. We ordered the spiced pumpkin candles, but the warehouse sent vanilla bean. Who even buys vanilla bean in October? It completely throws off the autumn aesthetic I had planned for the front window. I spent three hours sketching out that display on my iPad. My iPad screen actually has a crack in it right down the middle now... I dropped it when I was getting out of my car at the bank last month to try and confront the branch manager. The case I bought for it was supposed to be shatterproof, but that was a total lie. I should probably write a review online about that. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, it's definitely a coordinated effort. No amount of fake papers they try to print out and show me is going to change the fact that they are all working together to force me out of my home.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people who are not directly involved in your job or the foreclosure, like strangers or public figures, are also part of this effort against you?\n\nPatient: Strangers? Like celebrities or the mayor or something? No, that doesn't make any sense. Why would someone who doesn't even know me care about my house? It's just the people at the bank and my job who are doing this. \n\nThough speaking of strangers, the customers at the store this week have been completely out of their minds. This one lady came in yesterday demanding a full cash refund for a cardigan she bought three years ago. Three years! She didn't even have the tags or the receipt, and it smelled like mothballs. I had to call mall security because she just planted herself at my register and refused to move. The security guard, Todd, he's actually really nice about that stuff. He brought me a coffee last week from that new cafe down the street, the one with the dark green awning? Their espresso is incredibly bitter, though. I usually prefer making my own at home. I bought this really nice espresso machine on sale last Black Friday, but the milk frother attachment is already acting up. I tried calling their customer service line on my lunch break, and I was on hold for forty-five minutes. Forty-five minutes! Just listening to that awful, static-y elevator music. I don't even know why companies bother having a support line if no one is ever going to pick up the phone. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, public figures. But yeah, no, I haven't noticed anyone like that being involved. Just the people I actually deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something unusual was happening with your thoughts, like someone putting thoughts in or out of your mind, or that you had special powers or abilities that others don’t know about?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own, and I definitely don't have any special powers. I mean, if I had special powers, I probably would have used them to fix the schedule at work this week. \n\nMy assistant manager, Sarah, she completely messed up the shift rotations. She put three cashiers on during the slow morning shift and only one during the afternoon rush. It was a nightmare. I had to jump on a register myself for three hours straight, and the barcode scanner at register two is so finicky. You have to hold the tag at this exact, weird angle or it just beeps at you. Beep, beep, beep. It gave me such a headache. I ended up taking some ibuprofen from the first aid kit in the breakroom, but I think it was expired because it didn't help at all. I really need to remember to check the expiration dates on that stuff. Last time I looked, we still had a box of band-aids from like, 2018. They don't even stick anymore, the adhesive just turns into this gross, flaky powder. I tried to use one on a papercut I got from opening a shipment of those new denim jackets, and it just fell right off my finger onto the floor. \n\nSo yeah, no mind control or superpowers or whatever. Just me dealing with the same old incompetence from everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, objects, or actions in your environment had a special meaning specifically for you, even if others wouldn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think things have special secret meanings just for me. A coffee cup is just a coffee cup, you know? Like, I don't look at a billboard or a street lamp and think it's a secret message. \n\nAlthough, speaking of signs, the new promotional signage corporate sent us for the autumn clearance sale is completely ridiculous. They printed everything in this blinding neon orange color that clashes horribly with the store's overhead lighting. I told the district manager, Greg, that it makes the whole front section look like a highway construction zone. Greg never listens, though. He was supposed to come in for a walk-through on Tuesday, but he canceled at the last minute because his flight from Chicago got delayed. \n\nI absolutely hate flying. The last time I was on a plane, the guy next to me kept taking off his shoes, and I swear, his socks smelled exactly like stale corn chips. It was a four-hour flight to Orlando for my cousin's baby shower, and I couldn't even read my book because I was so nauseous. I ended up buying her this really expensive baby monitor off her registry, one of those ones with the HD video and room temperature sensors. It cost me like two hundred dollars, and then she didn't even send a thank-you note. Just a mass text to everyone with a picture of the baby's foot. I mean, who does that? It takes two seconds to write a card. I buy these beautiful embossed cards from this little stationery shop downtown, the one next to the bakery that makes those incredible almond croissants... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, special meanings. No, everything is just what it is. Nothing weird like that. Except the foreclosure papers, obviously, but that's just them being corrupt, not some magic symbol.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you had control or influence over other people’s actions or decisions, like you could affect how they behaved without them realizing it?\n\nPatient: Control people? No, definitely not. God, if I could control people, my life would be so much easier. I would definitely make my cashier, Jason, actually show up for his shifts on time. \n\nHe was twenty minutes late again this morning because he claimed he had to get gas. I mean, who waits until right before they have to be at work to get gas? You do it the night before. I always go to that Shell station on 4th Street because they usually have the cheapest unleaded, though their squeegees are always completely ruined. The sponge part is always ripped off, so it just scrapes the glass with the hard plastic. Screech, screech. It drives me crazy. I absolutely hate a dirty windshield, especially with the glare from the sun this time of year. The sun sets so early now, it's pitch black by the time I drive home. \n\nI actually had to change the headlight bulb on my Honda last weekend and it was a total nightmare. You practically have to take the whole front bumper off just to reach the housing unit. My hands were covered in grease for two days, no matter how much Dawn dish soap I used. Dawn is usually so good, too. I use it on everything, even laundry stains. If you mix it with a little baking soda, it takes tomato sauce right out of cotton. I spilled spaghetti on my favorite white blouse last month at that Italian place downtown—the one with the red checkered tablecloths? Their garlic bread is incredibly dry, honestly. I came out with my bags—I had just bought all the ingredients to make a lasagna, the really good kind with the ricotta cheese and the spicy Italian sausage. You have to brown the sausage first, obviously, but my sister always skips that step and just throws it in raw, and it makes the whole thing so greasy. She invited me over for dinner last Thanksgiving and the turkey was so dry it was like chewing on sawdust. She didn't even make her own cranberry sauce, just bought the canned stuff that keeps the shape of the tin when you slide it out. I hate that. \n\nAnyway, I came out of the grocery store and there was this massive dent right on the driver's side. Cost me four hundred dollars to get it popped out at the body shop on Elm Street. The guy there, Tony, he's actually really nice. He gave me a discount because I paid in cash. I always try to keep a little cash on me, just in case. You never know when the card readers are going to go down. Ours at the store went down for two hours last Black Friday, and people were absolutely losing their minds. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, a sense of doom. No, no random doom. Just the normal stress of keeping the store from falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that larger, organized groups, like government agencies or corporations, were specifically targeting you or involved in efforts against you?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, actually. That's exactly why I'm here. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just standard paperwork and missed payments. \n\nSpeaking of paperwork, the new inventory logs corporate wants us to fill out are a complete waste of time. We have to count every single item in the overstock bins manually now instead of just using the scanner. I was back there for three hours on Wednesday, and it is absolutely freezing in the stockroom because the weather stripping on the receiving door is completely rotted out. I told Greg we need a new rubber seal, but he just ignores me. He’s probably too busy eating those awful powdered donuts he always brings in. The white powder gets everywhere—all over his keyboard, all over the desk in the back office. It’s disgusting. I have to wipe the whole area down with Clorox wipes before I can even sit down to do the weekly schedule. I only buy the lemon-scented wipes, though, because the \"fresh breeze\" scent gives me a terrible headache. I get these awful tension headaches right behind my eyes, especially when the pollen count is high. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, groups targeting me. Yes. Like I said, it's not just missed payments like everyone claims. I know for a fact that the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. They've been coordinating this whole thing behind my back for almost two years now. They keep trying to hand me all these official-looking documents to prove I'm behind, but no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. I know what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that the evidence you're seeing might not actually support the idea that people are working against you, even though it feels that way to you?\n\nPatient: No, why would I? I told you, I know exactly what I've been seeing for the last two years. The bank, the loan servicer, my coworkers—they are all in on it. They're working together to push me out of my house, and I don't care how many fake documents they try to hand me about missed payments. \n\nSpeaking of missing things, Jason actually lost the keys to the display cases yesterday. Just completely misplaced them. We spent forty-five minutes looking for them, and they were sitting right on top of the microwave in the breakroom the whole time. Who puts keys on a microwave? It’s completely unsanitary. The inside of that microwave is a biohazard anyway because nobody ever covers their food. Someone exploded a Hot Pocket in there last week and just left it. The cheese was baked onto the glass plate like cement. I had to scrub it with one of those green Scotch-Brite pads, which I hate using because they ruin my manicures. \n\nI just got my nails done on Tuesday, this really pretty pale pink color. They want the property. \n\nIt’s a really beautiful lot, actually. I have these massive hydrangea bushes all along the front porch. You have to mix coffee grounds into the soil if you want the blooms to stay that really deep, vibrant blue. If the soil gets too alkaline, they turn pink, and I absolutely despise pink flowers in the front yard. It completely clashes with the brick on the house. I usually get my used coffee grounds from the Starbucks down the street—they give away those silver bags of espresso grounds for free if you ask. But the new manager there, this kid named Tyler, he started telling me I could only take one bag at a time. One bag! Do you know how big my flower beds are? \n\nTyler has this awful little mustache that looks like he drew it on with a Sharpie, and he always wears his green apron tied way too tight. It makes him look like a stuffed sausage. I complained to the district manager about him, but you know how food service is. They never fire anyone unless they actually steal from the register. Figured it out on my own, definitely. They would never be stupid enough to just tell me their plan directly. But I pay attention to how things unfold. I notice the little details that other people completely miss. 

It’s just like how I noticed our new cashier, Brittany, was stealing company time last week. She thought she was being so sneaky, clocking in on the app from the parking lot and then sitting in her car for fifteen minutes doing her makeup. I saw her right through the front display window. She uses this really cheap liquid eyeliner, too, the kind that completely flakes off by noon. I actually told her she needs to invest in a good waterproof gel liner, like the one I get from Sephora. It's thirty dollars, which is a lot, but it lasts for months. I actually bought my last one using a gift card my aunt got me for my birthday. My aunt always gets me gift cards because she says I'm impossible to shop for, which isn't even true. I just have very specific tastes. Like, I only wear sterling silver jewelry, never gold. Gold makes my skin look completely washed out, and it always leaves that weird metallic smell on your fingers. 

Wait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, if they told me what they were doing. 

No, of course not. But like I've been telling you, I'm not blind. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know what's really going on. I put the pieces together myself. The bank, the loan servicer, and even the people at my job—they have all been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I've watched it happen, step by step, for almost two years now. And honestly, no amount of papers they show me is ever going to change what I’ve been seeing with my own two eyes.

I know exactly what they're up to.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people working against you have ever communicated their intentions to you directly, or is it something you've figured out on your own based on how events have unfolded?\n\nPatient: Figured it out on my own, definitely.
